sepal_ui/sepalwidgets/sepal_ipyvuetify.py<#sep#> parameter t along BC. So coordinates of N can be written as (t*cos20°, t*sin20°), where t is between 0 and 1.

Similarly, point M is on AC, which goes from A(1,0) to C(0,0). Let's represent point M as (1 - s, 0), where s is between 0 and 1.

Now, angle BAN is 50°. Let's compute the coordinates of N such that this angle is achieved.

First, vector AN goes from A(1,0) to N(t*cos20°, t*sin20°). So the vector AN is (t*cos20° - 1, t*sin20° - 0) = (t*cos20° - 1, t*sin20°).

Vector AB goes from A(1,0) to B(cos20°, sin20°). So vector AB is (cos20° - 1, sin20°).

The angle between vectors AN and AB is 50°. So using the dot product formula:

cos(theta) = (AN . AB) / (|AN| |AB|)

Given theta = 50°, so:

( (t*cos20° - 1)(cos20° - 1) + (t*sin20°)(sin20°) ) / ( |AN| |AB| ) = cos50°

First, compute |AB|. Since AB is from (1,0) to (cos20°, sin20°), so length AB is sqrt( (cos20° - 1)^2 + (sin20°)^2 ). Let's compute:

(cos20° - 1)^2 + sin²20° = cos²20° - 2cos20° + 1 + sin²20° = (cos²20° + sin²20°) - 2cos20° + 1 = 1 - 2cos20° + 1 = 2 - 2cos20° = 4sin²10°, as before. So |AB| = 2sin10°, which is approximately 0.347.

Similarly, |AN| is the distance from A(1,0) to N(t*cos20°, t*sin20°):

sqrt( (t*cos20° - 1)^2 + (t*sin20°)^2 ) = sqrt( t²cos²20° - 2t cos20° + 1 + t²sin²20° ) = sqrt( t²(cos²20° + sin²20°) - 2t cos20° + 1 ) = sqrt( t² - 2t cos20° + 1 )

So the dot product:

AN . AB = (t*cos20° - 1)(cos20° - 1) + (t*sin20°)(sin20°)

Let's expand this:

= t*cos20°*cos20° - t*cos20° - cos20° + 1 + t*sin²20°

= t*(cos²20° + sin²20°) - t*cos20° - cos20° + 1

= t*(1) - t*cos20° - cos20° + 1

= t(1 - cos20°) - cos20° + 1

So, putting into the equation:

[ t(1 - cos20°) - cos20° + 1 ] / ( sqrt(t² - 2t cos20° + 1) * 2sin10° ) ) = cos50°

This is a bit complicated, but maybe we can solve for t.

Let me denote cos20° as c and sin10° as s for simplicity. Then we have:

[ t(1 - c) - c + 1 ] / ( sqrt(t² - 2tc + 1) * 2s ) ) = cos50°

But even with substitution, this seems complex. Maybe plugging in numerical values would help.

Compute numerical values:

cos20° ≈ 0.9397, sin10° ≈ 0.1736, cos50° ≈ 0.6428.

So equation becomes:

[ t(1 - 0.9397) - 0.9397 + 1 ] / ( sqrt(t² - 2t*0.9397 + 1) * 2*0.1736 ) ≈ 0.6428

Compute numerator:

t*(0.0603) + 0.0603 ≈ 0.0603(t + 1)

Denominator:

sqrt(t² - 1.8794t + 1) * 0.3472

So equation is:

0.0603(t + 1) / [ sqrt(t² - 1.8794t + 1) * 0.3472 ] ≈ 0.6428

Multiply both sides by denominator:

0.0603(t + 1) ≈ 0.6428 * 0.3472 * sqrt(t² - 1.8794t + 1)

Compute 0.6428 * 0.3472 ≈ 0.2232

So:

0.0603(t + 1) ≈ 0.2232 * sqrt(t² - 1.8794t + 1)

Divide both sides by 0.0603:

(t + 1) ≈ (0.2232 / 0.0603) * sqrt(t² - 1.8794t + 1)

Calculate 0.2232 / 0.0603 ≈ 3.7

So:

t + 1 ≈ 3.7 * sqrt(t² - 1.8794t + 1)

Square both sides:

(t + 1)^2 ≈ 13.69 * (t² - 1.8794t + 1)

Expand left side:

t² + 2t + 1 ≈ 13.69t² - 13.69*1.8794t + 13.69

Bring all terms to left side:

t² + 2t + 1 - 13.69t² + 13.69*1.8794t - 13.69 ≈ 0

Calculate coefficients:

-12.69t² + (2 + 13.69*1.8794)t - 12.69 ≈ 0

Compute 13.69*1.8794 ≈ 25.736

So:

-12.69t² + (2 + 25.736)t - 12.69 ≈ 0

Simplify:

-12.69t² + 27.736t - 12.69 ≈ 0

Multiply both sides by -1:

12.69t² - 27.736t + 12.69 ≈ 0

Now, solve quadratic equation:

t = [27.736 ± sqrt(27.736² - 4*12.69*12.69)] / (2*12.69)

Compute discriminant:

27.736² ≈ 769.1

4*12.69*12.69 ≈ 4*161.036 ≈ 644.144

So discriminant ≈ 769.1 - 644.144 ≈ 124.956

sqrt(124.956) ≈ 11.18

So t ≈ [27.736 ± 11.18] / 25.38

Two solutions:

t ≈ (27.736 + 11.18)/25.38 ≈ 38.916 / 25.38 ≈ 1.533 (discard, since t should be between 0 and 1)

t ≈ (27.736 - 11.18)/25.38 ≈ 16.556 / 25.38 ≈ 0.652

So t ≈ 0.652

Thus, coordinates of N are (0.652*cos20°, 0.652*sin20°)

Calculate cos20° ≈ 0.9397, sin20° ≈ 0.3420

So N ≈ (0.652*0.9397, 0.652*0.3420) ≈ (0.612, 0.223)

Similarly, find point M on AC such that angle ABM = 60°. Let's parametrize point M on AC. Since AC is from A(1,0) to C(0,0), let's let M be at (1 - s, 0), where s is between 0 and 1.

We need angle ABM = 60°. Let's compute the coordinates.

Vector BM goes from B(cos20°, sin20°) to M(1 - s, 0). So vector BM is (1 - s - cos20°, -sin20°)

Vector BA goes from B to A: (1 - cos20°, -sin20°)

The angle between vectors BM and BA is 60°. So using the dot product:

cos(theta) = (BM . BA) / (|BM| |BA| )

Given theta = 60°, so:

[ (1 - s - cos20°)(1 - cos20°) + (-sin20°)(-sin20°) ] / ( |BM| |BA| ) = cos60° = 0.5

Compute numerator:

= (1 - s - cos20°)(1 - cos20°) + sin²20°

Expand:

= (1 - cos20°)(1 - cos20° - s) + sin²20°

= (1 - cos20°)^2 - s(1 - cos20°) + sin²20°

= [1 - 2cos20° + cos²20°] - s(1 - cos20°) + sin²20°

Since cos²20° + sin²20° = 1, so this simplifies to:

1 - 2cos20° + 1 - s(1 - cos20°)

= 2 - 2cos20° - s(1 - cos20°)

So numerator: 2 - 2cos20° - s(1 - cos20°)

Denominator: |BM| |BA|

First, |BA| = 2 sin10°, as before.

|BM| is the distance from B(cos20°, sin20°) to M(1 - s, 0):

sqrt( (1 - s - cos20°)^2 + (0 - sin20°)^2 ) = sqrt( (1 - s - cos20°)^2 + sin²20° )

Let’s compute |BM|:

= sqrt( (1 - s - cos20°)^2 + sin²20° )

Expand (1 - s - cos20°)^2:

= (1 - cos20° - s)^2

= (1 - cos20°)^2 - 2(1 - cos20°)s + s²

So |BM| = sqrt( (1 - cos20°)^2 - 2(1 - cos20°)s + s² + sin²20° )

Again, using (1 - cos20°)^2 + sin²20° = 1 - 2cos20° + cos²20° + sin²20° = 2(1 - cos20°)

Thus,

|BM| = sqrt( 2(1 - cos20°) - 2(1 - cos20°)s + s² )

Let me factor out 2(1 - cos20°)(1 - s) + s²? Hmm, maybe not. Let's keep it as:

sqrt( s² - 2(1 - cos20°)s + 2(1 - cos20°) )

So denominator: sqrt(s² - 2(1 - cos20°)s + 2(1 - cos20°)) * 2 sin10°

Putting back into equation:

[2 - 2cos20° - s(1 - cos20°)] / [ sqrt(s² - 2(1 - cos20°)s + 2(1 - cos20°)) * 2 sin10° ] = 0.5

Multiply both sides by denominator:

2 - 2cos20° - s(1 - cos20°) = 0.5 * 2 sin10° * sqrt(s² - 2(1 - cos20°)s + 2(1 - cos20°))

Simplify:

Left side: 2(1 - cos20°) - s(1 - cos20°) = (1 - cos20°)(2 - s)

Right side: sin10° * sqrt(s² - 2(1 - cos20°)s + 2(1 - cos20°))

Let’s divide both sides by (1 - cos20°), assuming it's not zero, which it isn't (cos20° ≈ 0.94):

2 - s = (sin10° / (1 - cos20°)) * sqrt(s² - 2(1 - cos20°)s + 2(1 - cos20°))

Compute sin10° / (1 - cos20°):

Recall that 1 - cos20° = 2 sin²10°, so sin10° / (2 sin²10°) = 1/(2 sin10°). Therefore:

sin10° / (1 - cos20°) = 1/(2 sin10°)

Thus:

2 - s = (1/(2 sin10°)) * sqrt(s² - 2(1 - cos20°)s + 2(1 - cos20°))

Multiply both sides by 2 sin10°:

(2 - s) * 2 sin10° = sqrt(s² - 2(1 - cos20°)s + 2(1 - cos20°))

Square both sides:

[ (2 - s)^2 * 4 sin²10° ] = s² - 2(1 - cos20°)s + 2(1 - cos20°)

Expand left side:

4 sin²10° * (4 - 4s + s²) = s² - 2(1 - cos20°)s + 2(1 - cos20°)

Left side:

16 sin²10° - 16 sin²10° s + 4 sin²10° s²

Right side:

s² - 2(1 - cos20°)s + 2(1 - cos20°)

Bring all terms to left side:

16 sin²10° - 16 sin²10° s + 4 sin²10° s² - s² + 2(1 - cos20°)s - 2(1 - cos20°) = 0

Combine like terms:

(4 sin²10° s² - s²) + (-16 sin²10° s + 2(1 - cos20°)s) + (16 sin²10° - 2(1 - cos20°)) = 0

Factor:

s²(4 sin²10° - 1) + s(-16 sin²10° + 2(1 - cos20°)) + (16 sin²10° - 2(1 - cos20°)) = 0

Plug in numerical values:

sin10° ≈ 0.1736, so sin²10° ≈ 0.0301

cos20° ≈ 0.9397, so 1 - cos20° ≈ 0.0603

Now compute coefficients:

s² term: 4*0.0301 - 1 ≈ 0.1204 - 1 = -0.8796

s term: -16*0.0301 + 2*0.0603 ≈ -0.4816 + 0.1206 ≈ -0.361

constant term: 16*0.0301 - 2*0.0603 ≈ 0.4816 - 0.1206 ≈ 0.361

So equation becomes:

-0.8796 s² - 0.361 s + 0.361 = 0

Multiply through by -1:

0.8796 s² + 0.361 s - 0.361 = 0

Solve quadratic equation:

s = [ -0.361 ± sqrt(0.361² + 4*0.8796*0.361) ] / (2*0.8796)

Compute discriminant:

0.361² + 4*0.8796*0.361 ≈ 0.1303 + 1.266 ≈ 1.<#sep#> cryptic.

   162	        Check if the date is provided using the date format required for the widget.

   167	        Returns:

   170	        valid = True
   171	        try:
   172	            datetime.strptime(date, "%Y-%m-%d")
   173	        except (ValueError, TypeError):
   174	            valid = False

Multiply through by -1:

0.8796 s² + 0.361 s - 0.361 = 0

Solve quadratic equation:

s = [ -0.361 ± sqrt(0.361² + 4*0.8796*0.361) ] / (2*0.8796)

Compute discriminant:

0.361² + 4*0.8796*0.361 ≈ 0.1303 + 1.266 ≈ 1.3963

sqrt(1.3963) ≈ 1.1816

Thus:

s = [ -0.361 ± 1.1816 ] / 1.7592

Two solutions:

s = ( -0.361 + 1.1816 ) / 1.7592 ≈ 0.8206 / 1.7592 ≈ 0.467

s = ( -0.361 - 1.1816 ) / 1.7592 ≈ negative, discard.

So s ≈ 0.467

Thus, coordinates of M are (1 - s, 0) ≈ (1 - 0.467, 0) ≈ (0.533, 0)

Now, we have coordinates for points N ≈ (0.612, 0.223), M ≈ (0.533, 0), and B ≈ (cos20°, sin20°) ≈ (0.9397, 0.3420)

Now, need to find angle NMB, which is the angle at point M between points N and B.

To find angle NMB, we can compute the angle between vectors MN and MB.

First, find coordinates of vectors MN and MB.

Coordinates of MN: N - M = (0.612 - 0.533, 0.223 - 0) ≈ (0.079, 0.223)

Coordinates of MB: B - M = (0.9397 - 0.533, 0.3420 - 0) ≈ (0.4067, 0.3420)

Now, the angle between vectors MN and MB can be found using the dot product:

cos(theta) = (MN . MB) / (|MN| |MB| )

Compute the dot product:

(0.079)(0.4067) + (0.223)(0.3420) ≈ 0.0321 + 0.0763 ≈ 0.1084

Compute |MN|:

sqrt(0.079² + 0.223²) ≈ sqrt(0.0062 + 0.0497) ≈ sqrt(0.0559) ≈ 0.2365

Compute |MB|:

sqrt(0.4067² + 0.3420²) ≈ sqrt(0.1654 + 0.1169) ≈ sqrt(0.2823) ≈ 0.5314

So cos(theta) ≈ 0.1084 / (0.2365 * 0.5314) ≈ 0.1084 / 0.1256 ≈ 0.862

Therefore, theta ≈ arccos(0.862) ≈ 30.4 degrees

That's approximately 30 degrees. Considering the approximated calculations, this is likely 30 degrees. So the angle NMB is 30 degrees.

</think>To find \(\angle NMB\) in the given isosceles triangle \(ABC\) with \(AC = BC\) and \(\angle C = 20^\circ\), we proceed as follows:

1. **Identify the angles at \(A\) and \(B\)**:
   - Since \(AC = BC\), \(\angle BAC = \angle ABC = 80^\circ\).

2. **Determine the angles involving points \(M\) and \(N\)**:
   - Given \(\angle BAN = 50^\circ\), we have \(\angle NAC = 80^\circ - 50^\circ = 30^\circ\).
   - Given \(\angle ABM = 60^\circ\), we have \(\angle CBM = 80^\circ - 60^\circ = 20^\circ\).

3. **Coordinate Geometry Approach**:
   - Place \(C\) at the origin \((0,0)\), \(A\) at \((1,0)\), and \(B\) at \((\cos 20^\circ, \sin 20^\circ)\).

4. **Find Coordinates of \(N\) and \(M\)**:
   - For point \(N\) on \(BC\) such that \(\angle BAN = 50^\circ\), use the dot product to solve for \(t\):
     \[
     \cos 50^\circ = \frac{(t \cos 20^\circ - 1)(\cos 20^\circ - 1) + (t \sin 20^\circ)(\sin 20^\circ)}{\sqrt{(t \cos 20^\circ - 1)^2 + (t \sin 20^\circ)^2} \cdot 2 \sin 10^\circ}
     \]
     Solving this, we get \(t \approx 0.652\). Thus, \(N \approx (0.612, 0.223)\).

   - For point \(M\) on \(AC\) such that \(\angle ABM = 60^\circ\), use the dot product to solve for \(s\):
     \[
     \cos 60^\circ = \frac{(1 - s - \cos 20^\circ)(1 - \cos 20^\circ) + (\sin 20^\circ)(-\sin 20^\circ)}{\sqrt{(1 - s - \cos 20^\circ)^2 + (\sin 20^\circ)^2} \cdot 2 \sin 10^\circ}
     \]
     Solving this, we get \(s \approx 0.467\). Thus, \(M \approx (0.533, 0)\).

5. **Calculate Vectors and Angle \(\angle NMB\)**:
   - Vector \(MN = (0.612 - 0.533, 0.223 - 0) = (0.079, 0.223)\).
   - Vector \(MB = (\cos 20^\circ - 0.533, \sin 20^\circ - 0) = (0.4067, 0.3420)\).

   - Use the dot product to find the angle between \(MN\) and \(MB\):
     \[
     \cos \theta = \frac{(0.079)(0.4067) + (0.223)(0.3420)}{\sqrt{0.079^2 + 0.223^2} \cdot \sqrt{0.4067^2 + 0.3420^2}}
     \]
     \[
     \cos \theta \approx \frac{0.0321 + 0.0763}{0.2365 \cdot 0.5314} \approx \frac{0.1084}{0.1256} \approx 0.862
     \]

**Cop's Submission (Python, 33 bytes)**

**Program:**
```
for n in range(20):print(2**n-1)
```

**OEIS Sequence Generated:** Mersenne numbers: 2^n - 1. (OEIS A000225)

The program prints the Mersenne numbers for `n` from 0 to 19. 

---

**Reasoning:**
- The original code calculates 2^n - 1 for each `n`, which is OEIS A000225.
- A robber could crack this by deleting the '-1', resulting in **`print(2**n)`**, which outputs the powers of two (OEIS A000079). The challenge is to notice this simple subtraction and the straightforward modification.

---

**Marked Safe (After 7 Days):**

**Second Program (Modified):**
```
for n in range(20):print(2**n)
```

**New OEIS Sequence:** Powers of 2: a(n) = 2^n. (OEIS A000079)

The second program is obtained by removing the '-' and '1' characters from the initial code. This solution ensures the original sequence remains valid while allowing a small deletion to reach another valid sequence.<#sep#>   213

     \]<#sep#>human: Background
A maximal domino placement (MDP) on a rectangular grid is a non-overlapping placement of zero or more dominoes, so that no more dominoes can be added without overlapping some existing domino.
Alternatively, it can be thought of as a tiling using dominoes and monominoes (single square pieces) so that no two monominoes are adjacent to each other.
For example, the following are a few MDPs on a 3x3 grid: (-s and |s represent horizontal and vertical dominoes, and os represent holes respectively.)
--|   --|   --o
|o|   --|   o--
|--   --o   --o

There are exactly five MDPs on 2x3, and eleven on 2x4. Rotation and/or reflection of a placement is different from original unless they exactly coincide.
|||   |--   --|   o--   --o
|||   |--   --|   --o   o--

||||   ||--   |--|   |o--   |--o   --||
||||   ||--   |--|   |--o   |o--   --||

--o|   ----   o--|   o--o   ----
o--|   o--o   --o|   ----   ----

In the graph-theoretical sense, an MDP is equivalent to a maximal matching (maximal independent edge set) in the grid graph of given size.
Challenge
Given the width and height of a grid, count the number of distinct maximal domino placements on it.
Standard code-golf rules apply. The shortest code in bytes wins.
Test cases
A288026 is the table of values read by antidiagonals.
w|h| 1  2   3     4      5       6
---+------------------------------
1  | 1  1   2     2      3       4
2  | 1  2   5    11     24      51
3  | 2  5  22    75    264     941
4  | 2 11  75   400   2357   13407
5  | 3 24 264  2357  22228  207423
6  | 4 51 941 13407 207423 3136370

**Problem in clear terms**: The InputFile component currently allows unlimited upward navigation through directory structures. Users want to restrict this navigation to prevent getting lost in deep directory hierarchies by implementing a `max_depth` parameter.

- Parameter name: max_depth
- Parameter type: integer values
- Use cases: module_downloads, sepal_user

**Steps to reproduce the problem**:
1. Use InputFile component in an application

3. Observe there's no restriction on upward navigation

**Best practices for testing and fixing**:
- Ensure backward compatibility with existing code
- Make the parameter optional with sensible defaults
- Validate parameter inputs
